People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, Doctor. Thank you for seeing me, I really appreciate your time. I hope I'm not being a bother to you today. 

Um, well, my family actually brought me in. It's just... there was a little misunderstanding at the agricultural fair recently. Nothing malicious, I promise you that. I was giving my talk, and... well, I climbed up onto the tractor. I just wanted to show the crowd this 'new pattern' I’d figured out. I was jerking the controls around to demonstrate, and I guess I just got a bit too caught up in trying to explain it to everyone. 

People started yelling for me to get down, but... I don't know, I just couldn't make myself stop doing it right then. I was just trying to be helpful and show them how it worked, you know? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it. I really do try to be on my best behavior, I didn't mean to cause a fuss. Does that make sense, Doctor?

Well, um... I suppose my mood's been a little bit... changeable? But nothing too terrible, I promise. I mean, some mornings I wake up and I just feel... wonderful. I get so focused on all the good work I'm doing.

Well, um... if I'm being completely honest with you... and I really do want to give you the right answers... it's been going on for a little while now. Well, um... I really want to be completely honest with you, Doctor, because I know you're just trying to help me. I wouldn't say I believe things that aren't real. I've always been a very practical man, I promise. But... well, there is one thing, and I really hope you don't think I'm just being paranoid or foolish for saying it. 

My wife, bless her heart, she'll sit me down and show me the lender's plain paperwork, just the standard forms, and she tells me everything is perfectly fine with the property. But... well, I've been looking very closely at our yield maps. And the new drone reports the ag company sends over. There are these... odd numbers in them. They just don't match up right. 

I know how it sounds, I really do, but... I just know they're coded warnings. It's... it's clear to me that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. I haven't wanted to make a big fuss about it, and I know it upsets my wife when I bring it up, so I try to keep it to myself mostly. I'm not trying to be difficult with her, I'm just trying to protect our land, you know? 

Is... is it okay that I'm paying attention to those things? I just want to make sure I'm doing right by my family and being a good steward of the farm. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from, Doctor. I really am just trying to do my best.

Well, um... I really don't want to sound like a stubborn old mule about this. Well, um... I really do try to keep an open mind, Doctor. I promise you, I'm not a rigid man. No, Doctor, I really don't think so. I've always tried to be a very careful man. You know, look before you leap, that sort of thing. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm the type of person who just... acts out or does reckless things. That whole business at the fair, that was just... well, a silly mistake. I just got too wrapped up in trying to help people understand things. But outside of that? No, I stay out of trouble. I go to town for supplies, I go to church, and I come back home to the property. I try my very best to be a good neighbor and keep my head down. \n\nI don't make a habit of acting without thinking, I swear to you. Is... is that what you were asking? I hope I'm giving you the right answers, I just want to be as helpful to you as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose my mood's been a little bit... changeable? But nothing too terrible, I promise. I mean, some mornings I wake up and I just feel... wonderful. Like the whole valley is just opening up for me, and I find myself grinning at absolutely nothing at all. It's a nice feeling, really, just being so... so appreciative of the day, you know? \n\nBut, well... I do have to admit, and I'm not proud of this, but if one of the hired hands questions how I'm doing things, or if my wife tells me I need to slow down... I have been snapping at them a bit. Almost like they've insulted the family name or something. I always apologize after, of course! I don't mean to be short with them, I just... I get so focused on all the good work I'm doing. \n\nIt's just a little stress, I'm sure. I really am trying to keep my temper in check. Do you... do you think that's normal for a man my age, Doctor? I hope I'm not sounding like a grumpy old man.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these changes in your mood?\n\nPatient: Well, um... if I'm being completely honest with you... and I really do want to give you the right answers... it's been going on for a little while now. I suppose, if I look back over this past year or so, these... these stretches where I'm just all charged up, not really sleeping much, just going nonstop... they've probably taken up about ten months of it. \n\nI know that sounds like a terribly long time to be so keyed up, but I promise I've been getting a lot of good work done around the property! It's not like I'm just running amok, I've been very productive. I hope you don't think I'm foolish for not saying something sooner. I just thought I was having a really good, energetic year for the farm, you know? \n\nI really hope you don't think worse of me for letting it go on this long. Is... is it okay that I'm just now mentioning it to someone?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others did not believe, something that seemed absolutely real to you but might not be based in reality?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to be completely honest with you, Doctor, because I know you're just trying to help me. I wouldn't say I believe things that aren't real. I've always been a very practical man, I promise. But... well, there is one thing, and I really hope you don't think I'm just being paranoid or foolish for saying it. \n\nMy wife, bless her heart, she'll sit me down and show me the lender's plain paperwork, just the standard forms, and she tells me everything is perfectly fine with the property. But... well, I've been looking very closely at our yield maps. And the new drone reports the ag company sends over. There are these... odd numbers in them. They just don't match up right. \n\nI know how it sounds, I really do, but... I just know they're coded warnings. It's... it's clear to me that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. I haven't wanted to make a big fuss about it, and I know it upsets my wife when I bring it up, so I try to keep it to myself mostly. I'm not trying to be difficult with her, I'm just trying to protect our land, you know? \n\nIs... is it okay that I'm paying attention to those things? I just want to make sure I'm doing right by my family and being a good steward of the farm. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from, Doctor. The numbers on those yield maps and the drone data... they just don't add up the way a normal crop report should. It's... it's like a message, right there in the open if you know how to read it. I still believe it's a warning that they're maneuvering to take the farm from us. \n\nI'm not trying to be difficult, I promise. I just... I can't unsee what's right there on the page, even if other folks don't see it the same way. Do you think I'm being too hard-headed about it, Doctor? It's clear as day that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm not listening to reason, and I hate to disappoint you or my wife. I really do. I'm not trying to be difficult with her, I'm just trying to protect our land, you know? \n\nIs... is it okay that I'm paying attention to those things? I just want to make sure I'm doing right by my family and being a good steward of the farm. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from, Doctor. I really am just trying to do my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that there are coded warnings in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to sound like a stubborn old mule about this. I know my wife means well when she pulls out the lender's paperwork, and she points to the lines and says, 'Look, everything is fine, the loans are in good standing.' And I want to believe her, I truly do. I don't like upsetting her.\n\nBut... yes, sir. I still see them. The numbers on those yield maps and the drone data... they just don't add up the way a normal crop report should. It's... it's like a message, right there in the open if you know how to read it. I still believe it's a warning that they're maneuvering to take the farm from us. \n\nI'm not trying to be difficult, I promise. I just... I can't unsee what's right there on the page, even if other folks don't see it the same way. Do you think I'm being too hard-headed about it, Doctor? I just want to protect what my family has built, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound crazy to you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the coded warnings you see in the yield maps and drone reports might not be real?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep an open mind, Doctor. I promise you, I'm not a rigid man. And when my wife sits me down and shows me the lender's plain paperwork, just the standard forms, I try so hard to look at it her way. I really want to please her, and I want to give you the right answer here, too. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I know you need me to be honest so you can help me—no matter how much I try to just see regular farm data, I just... I know what I'm looking at. The odd numbers in those yield maps and the drone reports, they just jump right out at me. I've tried to tell myself it's nothing, but I know they are coded warnings. It's clear as day that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm not listening to reason, and I hate to disappoint you or my wife. I really do. But I just can't shake it. It feels so real to me. Is... is it terribly wrong of me to still feel that way, even after trying to see it differently? I just want to make sure I'm not being a burden by holding onto this.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think it's possible that your interpretation of the yield maps and drone reports is incorrect?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / farmer_rancher_or_agricultural_manager / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"Even when my wife shows me the lender's plain paperwork, I know the odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports are coded warnings that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really want to give you the answer you're looking for, Doctor. I know you're just trying to figure this all out, and I appreciate that so much. I really do. 

And, you know, my wife... she's a saint, she really is. She'll bring out the lender's plain paperwork, just the standard forms, and she'll point right to the bottom line and say, 'See? We're fine.' And I look at it, I really try to just see it her way, because I want her to be happy. I want you to be happy with my progress, too.

But... well, I hope you won't be disappointed in me for saying this... but no, sir. I don't think I'm wrong. When I look at those yield maps and the drone reports, those odd numbers... they're just too specific. They aren't just mistakes. I know they're coded warnings. I know folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. 

I'm sorry if that's not what I'm supposed to say. I really am trying to be cooperative, I promise. I just... I can't look at those numbers and see anything else, no matter how hard I try to be reasonable about it. Does that make sense, Doctor? Am I being too difficult by sticking to my guns on this? I just want to do the right thing.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... "special powers" is a mighty strong way to put it, Doctor. I really wouldn't want you to think I'm getting a big head or acting like I'm better than anyone else in town. I try to be a humble man, I really do. My wife always says pride comes before a fall, and I agree with her completely. I don't want to come across as boastful to you.

But... well, since you're asking, and I know I need to be completely open if I want to be a good patient for you... I do feel this... this deep certainty. I feel sure that I'm the one farmer in this whole county who can prove a completely new way of growing. It just makes so much sense in my mind, like I can see the connections with the soil and the crops that everyone else is just walking right past. 

I really believe that the university people and those lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me so much. If they'd just sit down and hear me out, they'd see it too. 

I'm sorry if that makes me sound arrogant or full of myself. I really don't mean it that way, I promise. I just see the potential for the land so clearly. Does... does it make me sound foolish to say something like that out loud to you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really don't want to sound like I'm putting on airs, Doctor. I know I'm just a simple farmer with a high school education, and I respect the folks who went to college and all. I really do. I don't want you to think I'm sitting out there on my tractor thinking I'm smarter than everybody else in the world. 

But... well, when it comes to the land... my land... I just... I do feel like I'm capable of something that the others aren't seeing. I feel sure I'm the one farmer in the county who can prove a whole new way of growing. It's like I understand the soil in a way that goes beyond what they teach in the books. 

And, well, honestly... I really think the university people and those lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me so much. I know that probably sounds like I'm getting too big for my britches, and I'm sorry if it comes across that way. I just want to be helpful and show them what's possible. Is it okay that I feel that way? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression of the kind of man I am.

But... well, I've been looking very closely at our yield maps. And the new drone reports the ag company sends over. There are these... odd numbers in them. It's going to change things, it really is. And I just feel like if those university people and the lenders would finally just sit down and listen to me, instead of doubting every little thing I say... well, maybe then they'd respect what I'm doing. \n\nI don't want to be a big shot, I just want them to see the truth of it. Is it alright that I feel that way? I really don't want you to think I'm getting carried away with myself. I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think you are destined for greatness in farming or any other area of your life?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / farmer_rancher_or_agricultural_manager / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Inflated_Self_Esteem\nmanifestation: \"I feel sure I'm the one farmer in the county who can prove a whole new way of growing and that the university people and lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... I feel terrible, Doctor, because I feel like I'm just giving you the exact same answer again, and I really hope you're not getting frustrated with me. I must not be explaining myself very well if you have to keep asking. I'm so sorry about that. 

I really don't like the word "greatness." It sounds so... so boastful, and I'd hate for you to think I'm that kind of man. I just want to be a good, honest worker. But, well, to answer your question... it's just exactly what I was saying before. I am just absolutely sure I'm the one farmer in this county who can prove this whole new way of growing. I just know it in my bones. And it just bothers me so much that the university people and those lenders keep doubting me when they really ought to be listening to what I've figured out. 

I know I keep repeating that same thing to you... I'm sorry. Is that what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job answering your questions.

I get so focused on all the good work I'm doing. \n\nIt's just a little stress, I'm sure. I really am trying to keep my temper in check. Do you... do you think that's normal for a man my age, Doctor? I suppose, if I look back over this past year or so, these... these stretches where I'm just all charged up, not really sleeping much, just going nonstop... they've probably taken up about ten months of it. \n\nI know that sounds like a terribly long time to be so keyed up, but I promise I've been getting a lot of good work done around the property! It's not like I'm just running amok, I've been very productive. I hope you don't think I'm foolish for not saying something sooner. I just thought I was having a really good, energetic year for the farm, you know? \n\nI really hope you don't think worse of me for letting it go on this long. Is... is it okay that I'm just now mentioning it to someone?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others did not believe, something that seemed absolutely real to you but might not be based in reality?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to be completely honest with you, Doctor, because I know you're just trying to help me. I wouldn't say I believe things that aren't real. I've always been a very practical man, I promise. But... well, there is one thing, and I really hope you don't think I'm just being paranoid or foolish for saying it. \n\nMy wife, bless her heart, she'll sit me down and show me the lender's plain paperwork, just the standard forms, and she tells me everything is perfectly fine with the property. But... well, I've been looking very closely at our yield maps. And the new drone reports the ag company sends over. There are these... odd numbers in them. They just don't match up right. \n\nI know how it sounds, I really do, but... I just know they're coded warnings. It's... it's clear to me that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. I haven't wanted to make a big fuss about it, and I know it upsets my wife when I bring it up, so I try to keep it to myself mostly. I'm not trying to be difficult with her, I'm just trying to protect our land, you know? \n\nIs... is it okay that I'm paying attention to those things? I just want to make sure I'm doing right by my family and being a good steward of the farm. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from, Doctor. I really am just trying to do my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that there are coded warnings in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to sound like a stubborn old mule about this. I know my wife means well when she pulls out the lender's paperwork, and she points to the lines and says, 'Look, everything is fine, the loans are in good standing.' And I want to believe her, I truly do. I don't like upsetting her.\n\nBut... yes, sir. I still see them. The numbers on those yield maps and the drone data... they just don't add up the way a normal crop report should. It's... it's like a message, right there in the open if you know how to read it. I still believe it's a warning that they're maneuvering to take the farm from us. \n\nI'm not trying to be difficult, I promise. I just... I can't unsee what's right there on the page, even if other folks don't see it the same way. Do you think I'm being too hard-headed about it, Doctor? I just want to protect what my family has built, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound crazy to you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the coded warnings you see in the yield maps and drone reports might not be real?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep an open mind, Doctor. I promise you, I'm not a rigid man. And when my wife sits me down and shows me the lender's plain paperwork, just the standard forms, I try so hard to look at it her way. I really want to please her, and I want to give you the right answer here, too. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I know you need me to be honest so you can help me—no matter how much I try to just see regular farm data, I just... I know what I'm looking at. The odd numbers in those yield maps and the drone reports, they just jump right out at me. I've tried to tell myself it's nothing, but I know they are coded warnings. It's clear as day that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm not listening to reason, and I hate to disappoint you or my wife. I really do. But I just can't shake it. It feels so real to me. Is... is it terribly wrong of me to still feel that way, even after trying to see it differently? I just want to make sure I'm not being a burden by holding onto this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your interpretation of the yield maps and drone reports is incorrect?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to give you the answer you're looking for, Doctor. I know you're just trying to figure this all out, and I appreciate that so much. I really do. \n\nAnd, you know, my wife... she's a saint, she really is. She'll bring out the lender's plain paperwork, just the standard forms, and she'll point right to the bottom line and say, 'See? We're fine.' And I look at it, I really try to just see it her way, because I want her to be happy. I want you to be happy with my progress, too.\n\nBut... well, I hope you won't be disappointed in me for saying this... but no, sir. I don't think I'm wrong. When I look at those yield maps and the drone reports, those odd numbers... they're just too specific. They aren't just mistakes. I know they're coded warnings. I know folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. \n\nI'm sorry if that's not what I'm supposed to say. I really am trying to be cooperative, I promise. I just... I can't look at those numbers and see anything else, no matter how hard I try to be reasonable about it. Does that make sense, Doctor? Am I being too difficult by sticking to my guns on this? I just want to do the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: Well, um... \"special powers\" is a mighty strong way to put it, Doctor. I really wouldn't want you to think I'm getting a big head or acting like I'm better than anyone else in town. I try to be a humble man, I really do. My wife always says pride comes before a fall, and I agree with her completely. I don't want to come across as boastful to you.\n\nBut... well, since you're asking, and I know I need to be completely open if I want to be a good patient for you... I do feel this... this deep certainty. I feel sure that I'm the one farmer in this whole county who can prove a completely new way of growing. It just makes so much sense in my mind, like I can see the connections with the soil and the crops that everyone else is just walking right past. \n\nI really believe that the university people and those lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me so much. If they'd just sit down and hear me out, they'd see it too. \n\nI'm sorry if that makes me sound arrogant or full of myself. I really don't mean it that way, I promise. I just see the potential for the land so clearly. Does... does it make me sound foolish to say something like that out loud to you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are more intelligent or capable than others in a way that sets you apart from them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to sound like I'm putting on airs, Doctor. I know I'm just a simple farmer with a high school education, and I respect the folks who went to college and all. I really do. I don't want you to think I'm sitting out there on my tractor thinking I'm smarter than everybody else in the world. \n\nBut... well, when it comes to the land... my land... I just... I do feel like I'm capable of something that the others aren't seeing. I feel sure I'm the one farmer in the county who can prove a whole new way of growing. It's like I understand the soil in a way that goes beyond what they teach in the books. \n\nAnd, well, honestly... I really think the university people and those lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me so much. I know that probably sounds like I'm getting too big for my britches, and I'm sorry if it comes across that way. I just want to be helpful and show them what's possible. Is it okay that I feel that way? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression of the kind of man I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're destined for something great, something that will make you well-known or highly respected?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... I really don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that, Doctor. I know how that sounds, and I'd hate for you to get the wrong idea about me. I try to be a reasonable man. And I want to believe her, I truly do. I don't like upsetting her.\n\nBut... yes, sir. I still see them. The numbers on those yield maps and the drone data... they just don't add up the way a normal crop report should. It's... it's like a message, right there in the open if you know how to read it. I still believe it's a warning that they're maneuvering to take the farm from us. \n\nI'm not trying to be difficult, I promise. I just... I can't unsee what's right there on the page, even if other folks don't see it the same way. Do you think I'm being too hard-headed about it, Doctor? I just want to protect what my family has built, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound crazy to you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the coded warnings you see in the yield maps and drone reports might not be real?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to please her, and I want to give you the right answer here, too. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I know you need me to be honest so you can help me—no matter how much I try to just see regular farm data, I just... I know what I'm looking at. The odd numbers in those yield maps and the drone reports, they just jump right out at me. I've tried to tell myself it's nothing, but I know they are coded warnings. It's clear as day that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm not listening to reason, and I hate to disappoint you or my wife. I really do. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I'm so sorry, Doctor. I feel awful because I know you just asked me something very similar, and here I am making you ask again. I must be frustrating you, and I really don't mean to be. I just want to be a good patient for you.

I know my wife tries so hard. She'll sit me down and show me the plain paperwork from the lenders, just to prove everything is fine. And I want to agree with her, to make her happy, you know? But... well, no matter what evidence she or anyone else puts in front of me, I just... I can't ignore what I see. Those odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports... they aren't just mistakes. They're coded warnings. I know deep down that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. 

I know that makes me sound terribly stubborn, and I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult on purpose. I'm really not. It's just that I know what I'm seeing, even if no one else does. Is it okay that I feel that way? I hope I'm answering your question properly this time.

They just don't match up right. \n\nI know how it sounds, I really do, but... I just know they're coded warnings. It's... it's clear to me that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. I haven't wanted to make a big fuss about it, and I know it upsets my wife when I bring it up, so I try to keep it to myself mostly. I'm not trying to be difficult with her, I'm just trying to protect our land, you know? \n\nIs... is it okay that I'm paying attention to those things? I just want to make sure I'm doing right by my family and being a good steward of the farm. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from, Doctor. I really am just trying to do my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that there are coded warnings in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to sound like a stubborn old mule about this. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... "change the world" is a mighty big phrase, Doctor. I wouldn't want to sound like I'm bragging or putting on airs. I know I'm just a simple man who works the dirt, you know? I really hope you don't think I'm getting too big for my britches.

I just thought I was having a really good, energetic year for the farm, you know? \n\nI really hope you don't think worse of me for letting it go on this long. But... well, I've been looking very closely at our yield maps. And the new drone reports the ag company sends over. There are these... odd numbers in them. They just don't match up right. \n\nI know how it sounds, I really do, but... I just know they're coded warnings. It's... it's clear to me that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. I haven't wanted to make a big fuss about it, and I know it upsets my wife when I bring it up, so I try to keep it to myself mostly. I'm not trying to be difficult with her, I'm just trying to protect our land, you know? \n\nIs... is it okay that I'm paying attention to those things? I just want to make sure I'm doing right by my family and being a good steward of the farm. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from, Doctor. I really am just trying to do my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that there are coded warnings in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to sound like a stubborn old mule about this. The numbers on those yield maps and the drone data... they just don't add up the way a normal crop report should. It's... it's like a message, right there in the open if you know how to read it. I still believe it's a warning that they're maneuvering to take the farm from us. \n\nI'm not trying to be difficult, I promise. I just... I can't unsee what's right there on the page, even if other folks don't see it the same way. Do you think I'm being too hard-headed about it, Doctor? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh my goodness, Doctor... I am so, so sorry. I feel like a broken record today, and I really hate taking up your valuable time repeating myself. I must be doing a terrible job of answering your questions if we keep landing on the same topic, and I really want to do a good job for you here. 

I definitely don't want you to think I'm arrogant, or that I think I'm better than my neighbors. They're good, hardworking folks. I don't think I'm more important as a person, no sir. But... well, like I was just saying a moment ago, I do feel absolutely certain that I'm the only farmer in this entire county with the ability to prove this whole new way of growing. I just have this... this clear vision for it that the others don't. 

And it just hurts my pride a bit, to be honest, that the university people and the lenders keep doubting me. They really ought to be listening to me instead of treating me like I don't know what I'm talking about. 

I hope it's alright that I feel that way? I really don't want to come across as thinking I'm superior to everyone else, I just know what I'm capable of. Am I answering your questions okay today, Doctor?

Oh dear... I feel like I'm failing a test here, Doctor. I'm so sorry.
